# Requirement cross-reference and requirement-to-test matrix

Status legend: **Verified** = implemented and tested in this repository today (file cited); **Proposed** = engineering proposal from the specification or from this document, awaiting operator approval; **Pending decision** = needs an operator answer (owner named from spec §6.6). "No answer yet" never becomes "not applicable" (§6.6).

Last derived from specification v2.0 (2026-10-22). Source: `docs/spec/master-specification.md` (repository copy supplied 2026-10-23). Repository state re-verified on 2026-10-24; sections 1 to 4, 6 and 7 regenerated from the committed test titles on 2026-10-30 (section 3).

Package A deliverable "Requirement cross-reference" (§6.5) and handoff artifact "Requirement-to-test matrix" (§6.7). Requirement conventions and lifecycle: §1.5.

## 1. What this document is

- One row for every requirement identifier in the specification: 139 `SPK-<DOMAIN>-###` requirements, the 12 numbered integrity invariants of §4.7, and the 28 `SPK-AT-###` acceptance journeys of §6.1. Total: 179 rows. (**Verified**: counted with `grep -oE 'SPK-[A-Z]+-[0-9]{3}' docs/spec/master-specification.md | sort -u` on the repository copy, 167 distinct ids; the §4.7 list was counted by hand.)
- For each row: the owning module, the lifecycle stage reached today, the evidence for that stage, the delivery package, the decision status, and the legacy identifier column required by §1.5.
- A requirement-to-test matrix organised by test file (section 6: one row per test file whose `it`/`describe` titles name a requirement id or a §4.7 invariant, 398 of the 476 committed test files on 2026-10-30), and the maintenance rules (section 10).

The "original platform specification" (`.docx`, §1.3 item 1) and the "existing local demonstration" (§1.4) are **not** in this repository. No legacy requirement identifier and no demo behaviour is cited anywhere in this document; see section 8.

## 2. How to read the columns

**Id** and **Spec §**: exactly as the specification numbers them. The §4.7 invariants have no `SPK` identifier; they are referenced as "§4.7 (n)".

**Title / short statement**: a compression of the requirement text. The specification remains the authoritative wording.

**Owning module** (§4.2 and `apps/api/src/modules/<module>/README.md`):

- Where a module README names the section or identifier, the owner is as written in that README (**Verified** as a statement of what the README says). All fourteen domain modules are implemented (each README describes its module as implemented; re-checked 2026-10-30, section 3).
- Where no README names it, the owner is marked "(proposed)" and is this document's **Proposed** assignment.
- Customer app and staff dashboard surfaces (§2) have no module owner in §4.2; they are Package E clients in `apps/mobile` and `apps/staff` (per-screen state in their READMEs), served by the API modules.

**Lifecycle stage** (§1.5: Specified, Implemented, Verified, Operator accepted, Released). Values used today:

| Value                            | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Specified                        | The requirement exists only in the specification. No module code in this repository implements any of it.                                                                                                                                                                |
| Implemented (kernel slice)       | A shared-kernel or shared-package artefact implements one named clause of the requirement and is exercised only by a generic test (not a test named after the requirement). The clause is named in the evidence column. Everything else in the requirement is Specified. |
| Implemented (module slice)       | A module implements one named clause and cites the id in its source (regeneration step 3, section 10.2), but no passing test is named after the id; the evidence names the source file and any generic test that exercises the clause. Everything else is Specified.     |
| Verified (kernel slice)          | A test in `apps/api/src/kernel`, `packages/contracts` or `packages/db` that names the requirement id, or tests its exact clause, passes today. The test file is cited. Everything else in the requirement is Specified.                                                  |
| Verified (module slice)          | A test in the owning module named after the requirement id passes today and covers the clauses it names; the evidence column names the clauses that remain Specified (usually because they belong to a scaffold module).                                                 |
| Verified (module)                | Tests in the owning module named after the requirement id pass today and, together with any kernel or contracts test, cover every clause of the requirement as the specification words it. No clause remains Specified. The requirement has not been operator accepted.  |
| Verified (assembled application) | A cross-module journey in `apps/api/src/__tests__/` named after the id passes; no module test is named after it. Clause coverage as for a slice.                                                                                                                         |
| Verified (client slice)          | Only a jest or jsdom test in `apps/mobile`, `apps/staff` or `packages/api-client` is named after the id. This is not native or workstation acceptance (§6.2, §6.3), and no API test is named after the id.                                                               |
| Operator accepted, Released      | No row has reached these stages.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

**Evidence**: the test file(s) and test title(s) that justify the stage. "none yet" means no code or test exists for the row. Test titles are quoted verbatim from the files.

**Package** (§6.5, A to G): the package whose deliverable list names the capability. Where §6.5 does not name the capability, the cell carries `*` and the assignment is this document's **Proposed** reading; section 9 lists each such case. The delivery sequence as a whole is the specification's proposal.

**Status**: **Verified** where the evidence column cites a passing test (the marker applies to the slice the lifecycle column names; for "Verified (module)" it applies to the whole requirement). **Proposed** where a row or a remaining clause depends on no open operator decision. **Pending decision (owner)** where the row's behaviour depends on a §6.6 decision; the owner is the "Required owner" from the §6.6 table, and the `DEC-###` identifier, where cited, is the record in `docs/package-a/decision-register.md`.

**Legacy id**: "pending: original specification not in repository" on every row (section 8).

## 3. Evidence base for every "Verified" statement in this document

**Verified 2026-10-30 (correction):** when this matrix was regenerated the root README recorded the stage-4 run at the integration branch: `vitest run` 3018 tests in 389 files, passing on PGlite and on PostgreSQL through `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, and the customer app's jest suite 1113 tests in 87 suites. The README now records the stage-5 run (3101 tests in 391 files; 1142 jest tests in 87 suites); the tests the stage-5 fixes added to existing files are not indexed in sections 4 and 6, which were not regenerated for them. This regeneration did not re-run the suite; it read the 476 committed test files (`git ls-files` of `*.spec.ts`, `*.test.ts`, `*.test.tsx`) and indexed the ids their `it`/`describe` titles name. The 2026-10-24 run recorded here earlier had 54 test files and 337 tests.

**Verified**: code present, by inspection of the tree on 2026-10-30 (the 2026-10-24 inspection found six migrations, five implemented modules and nine scaffolds):

- The shared kernel (`apps/api/src/kernel/`), the shared contracts package (`packages/contracts/src/`, one `api/<module>.ts` per module) and the database package with 120 migrations (`0001_core_schema.sql` to `1480_automation_org_scoped_kernel_fks.sql`) and one Kysely schema file per module under `packages/db/src/schema/`.
- All fourteen domain modules are implemented: each owns tables, commands, ports and events, and its README names its tests.
- No managed provider adapter exists (§5.2): every provider runs behind a port with a deterministic fake (`docs/package-a/integration-register.md` section 3.6), the only identity provider is the local development one (`AUTH_MODE=local`, refused in production), and no vendor SDK appears in any `package.json`. The customer app (`apps/mobile`) and the staff dashboard (`apps/staff`) exist and are tested in jest and jsdom only.
- `docs/api/openapi.json` (generated by `pnpm build && pnpm openapi`) contains 575 operations on 480 paths, equal to the served routes (`apps/api/src/__tests__/openapi-parity.spec.ts`).

The column "Requirement ids named in titles" is computed from the `describe` and `it` titles only (regeneration step 2, section 10.2); ids that appear in a comment or in source but not in a test title are candidates for "Implemented", never "Verified" (step 3).

Requirement ids with a passing test named after them today, by the module whose tests prove them:

| §4.7 (1)  | No unauthorised cross-household or cross-organisation access | §4.7   | kernel `AuthorizationService`; identity (relationship authority, household as grouping only); every module's composite foreign keys                                                                         | Verified (kernel and module slice) | Organisation scope: `apps/api/src/kernel/__tests__/authorization.spec.ts` "never crosses organization scope, even for staff and system actions", "allows staff by role and enforces facility scope", "reports protected denials as not-found and unprotected denials as access denied", "system principals are allowed within their organization only"; `auth-and-operations.spec.ts` "shows an operation to its requester and to staff, and hides it across accounts and organizations"; database rejection by composite foreign keys in every schema: `packages/db/src/core-schema.spec.ts` "facility references cannot cross organizations (composite foreign key)", `identity-schema.spec.ts` "SPK-IAM-001 / §4.3: a contact point cannot reference a person of another organization", "SPK-IAM-001: one live relationship per (actor, subject, kind); cross-organization pairs are rejected", `catalog-schema.spec.ts` "§4.3: an offering cannot reference another organization's program, policy or manager", scheduling and facilities `schema-invariants.spec.ts` "§4.3: references cannot cross organizations (composite foreign keys)", `competition-schema.spec.ts` "§4.3: a roster row, a team role or an entry can never point at another organization"; read paths: `principal-resolver.spec.ts` "cross-organization: a token from another organization never sees this organization's people", `competitions-seasons.spec.ts` "§4.3 / §4.10: records of another organization are not found, even for a league manager there". ### 4.24 SPK-AT, end-to-end acceptance journeys (§6.1)

Journeys are cross-module. "Modules involved" lists the modules the scenario touches; the first listed is the proposed coordinator. The package is the earliest package in which the journey can be executed end to end (**Proposed** reading of §6.5). A journey reaches "Verified (module)" only when a test named after its id passes and covers the journey's required outcome through the API; today such tests live in the module `__tests__` directories (section 10.1, item 3). Operator acceptance (§6.3) is recorded for no journey.

## 6. Requirement-to-test matrix (by test file)

**Regenerated 2026-10-30.** One row per committed test file whose `it`, `describe` or `test` titles name a requirement id (including `SPK-X-001/002` and `SPK-X-001..004` forms) or a §4.7 invariant ("§4.7 invariant 8", "§4.7-12", "invariants 2, 3, 7"): 398 of the 476 test files. The 2026-10-24 version listed every test title verbatim for 54 files; at today's size (more than 4,000 tests) the verbatim titles are produced by regeneration step 2b (section 10.2) rather than kept here. Section 4 quotes, for every row, up to three of the titles verbatim. A file that touches a clause without naming the id in a title never moves that row to Verified (section 10.1).

## 8. Legacy identifiers

§1.5: "Existing requirement identifiers from the original specification remain valid. Implementation work must maintain a cross-reference between those requirements and this expanded specification rather than renumbering or silently dropping them."

**Verified**: the original platform specification (`.docx`, §1.3 item 1, which the specification links at a path on its author's machine) is not in this repository, and no file in the repository contains its identifiers. The repository note at the top of `docs/spec/master-specification.md` says the same.

Consequently every "Legacy id" cell reads "pending: original specification not in repository". The legacy identifier scheme itself is unknown here: this document does not guess its prefix, numbering or count.

**Pending decision** (Sponsor, as holder of "Budget, dates, acceptance authority" in §6.6, or whoever the sponsor names as document owner): supply the original specification to the repository. When it arrives, section 8 gains a two-column mapping (legacy id to `SPK` id or "no successor, reason") and every row's legacy column is filled from it; the `SPK` ids are never renumbered.

The "existing local demonstration" (§1.4) is likewise absent, so the §4.1 parity tests and the remaining §4.5 shortcut replacements cannot be assessed against it; `docs/architecture/overview.md` records the same gap.

## 9. Interpretation notes and spec ambiguities affecting this matrix

Each item is **Proposed** unless marked otherwise. Package cells carrying `*` in section 4 trace to items 1 to 4.

1. **Capabilities §6.5 does not place in a package.** Discipline (SPK-DISC-001..005, SPK-AT-013), placement (SPK-PLACE-001..003, 005), staff dashboard navigation and Today workspace (SPK-UX-005, SPK-UX-006), youth trials and makeups (SPK-YOUTH-001..004, SPK-AT-019), disputed access and duplicates (SPK-IAM-005, SPK-IAM-007), and all of reporting except retention (SPK-REPORT-001..004, 006, 007). Assigned to the package whose gate first needs them. **Pending decision** (Sponsor: delivery commitment; Product owner for conditional items) to confirm.
2. **"Pickup" has two meanings.** Package E lists "Pickup when applicable" beside "Arrival and attendance"; Package G lists "Pickup" among conditional capabilities; §1.6 names "supervised pickup" as conditional; §2.2 Discover and SPK-PLACE-004 use "pickup" for pickup sessions. This document reads E as supervised pickup (SPK-ENTRY-007) and G as pickup sessions (SPK-PLACE-004); the catalog `pickup` offering kind belongs to the second meaning. **Pending decision** (Product owner: conditional capability applicability).
3. **Fixture generation ownership.** The scheduling README (re-read 2026-10-24) claims only SPK-FIX-005 of §3.8 and states that "the generator and SPK-FIX-001..004 data live in competition"; §4.4 lists Fixture under "Matches" and the competition README owns fixtures while deferring the generator and `FIXTURE_OBLIGATION_IMPL` beyond part 1. Rows SPK-FIX-001..004 therefore name competition as primary owner and scheduling as co-owner for publication: the scheduling `publications` row records the remaining obligations that SPK-FIX-002 requires (Implemented slice), and SPK-FIX-005 is proven on the scheduling side (`events.spec.ts`, `schedule-drafts.spec.ts`) and on the competition side (`fixtures.spec.ts`).
4. **Price rules are claimed twice.** The catalog README cites SPK-PAY-001..003 (price rules and discount definitions; "the quote itself belongs to finance") and the finance README cites SPK-PAY-001..012. Rows name catalog as the implemented owner of price rules and discount definitions (**Verified** slices and SPK-PAY-003 whole) and finance as owner of quotes, orders, obligations and allocation at checkout (Specified). This split is consistent with `docs/architecture/domain-model.md` as read on 2026-10-24.
5. **§3.16 has no module owner** in any README. Owners for SPK-COND-001..004 are this document's proposal; the catalog README states that membership and package offerings are catalogued but their entitlement effects are not implemented there.
6. **Customer and staff surfaces have no module owner.** §4.2 has no interface module; SPK-UX rows name the surface and the data-owning modules.
7. **SPK-YOUTH-005 development records** are coach-authored assessments; the enrollment README claims all of §3.13 and the catalog README claims the configuration half of SPK-YOUTH-001/002. Rows follow the READMEs. An alternative owner for SPK-YOUTH-005 (workforce, as the module owning coach assignments) is noted for the operator's module-ownership review.
8. **SPK-AT-020 spans two packages** (makeups in D, packages in G).
9. **§6.5 Package C does not name SPK-UX-007** (coach and referee workspace); Package E does. Row placed in E.
10. **Rounding** in `packages/contracts/src/money.ts` (half away from zero) and the per-definition rounding modes of the catalog discount evaluator are engineering defaults that tests exercise; §6.6 reserves rounding to Finance (DEC-007). The SPK-PAY-003 row is **Verified** for the mechanism and **Pending decision** for the values.
11. **Test evidence in PGlite mode only** was produced for this document; the PostgreSQL job (`test-postgres`) exists in CI but no run is recorded in the repository. Rows whose invariant is concurrency-sensitive (§4.7 (3), (4), (5), (6), (7); SPK-AT-005, 007, 008, 020) carry "Verified" only for what a serialized run can prove (constraint rejection, group atomicity, replay safety); the two-transaction race for §4.7 (3), §4.7 (4) and SPK-AT-008 stays **Proposed** until a `test-postgres` run is recorded in `docs/package-a/release-evidence-ledger.md` (CLAUDE.md "Tests").
12. **"Verified (module)" versus "Verified (module slice)".** The whole-requirement marker is applied only where the owning module's tests cover every clause of the specification's wording (section 2). Nineteen rows qualify; where a clause depends on a scaffold module (finance prices, communications notices, enrollment counting, discipline suspensions) the row stays a slice and names the clause. This is this document's reading of §1.5 "Verified"; the operator's acceptance (§6.3) is a separate, later stage.
13. **Journeys proven inside module tests.** SPK-AT-002, 003, 004, 008, 009, 013, 021 and 025 are proven by tests inside the owning modules' `__tests__` directories, not by a dedicated journeys directory (section 10.1, item 3). SPK-AT-021 is the one journey whose required outcome is covered end to end through the API of both modules it names; the others cover the clauses the implemented modules own.
14. **The competition module is "part 1".** Its README defers results, standings, brackets, discipline, disputes, the readiness evaluator and the fixture generator to part 2. SPK-COMP and SPK-CAP rows are therefore slices or wholes of part 1 only; SPK-RESULT, SPK-DISC and SPK-READY rows remain Specified or Implemented. `docs/api/openapi.json` has not been regenerated since the competition contracts were added (section 3).
15. **Decision identifiers.** Where a test title or README names a `DEC-###` record (DEC-002, DEC-048, DEC-064), the Status column carries it beside the §6.6 owner. DEC-064 ("tentative" RSVP) has no exact §6.6 row; the decision register assigns it to Product and operations, and this document follows the register.

## 10. How this matrix is maintained

### 10.1 Rules

1. **One row per requirement id, forever.** The row inventory is the set of ids in `docs/spec/master-specification.md` plus the numbered §4.7 list plus the §6.1 table. Rows are never deleted or renumbered (§1.5). A requirement withdrawn by a later specification version keeps its row with lifecycle "Specified" and a note naming the version that withdrew it. (**Proposed** rule; consistent with §1.5.)
2. **Tests are named after requirement ids** (CLAUDE.md, "Tests": "Every requirement you implement gets a test named after it; every integrity invariant gets a test that proves the database rejects the violation, not only the service."). The practice in force today (**Verified**): the id appears in the `describe` or `it` title, usually as a leading prefix, for example `it('SPK-FAC-005: generation materializes tasks and future restrictions through the scheduling authority, exactly once per occurrence')` in `apps/api/src/modules/facilities/__tests__/preventive-schedules.spec.ts`, `it('§4.7 (3): division capacity is enforced by the database on placement')` in `apps/api/src/modules/competition/__tests__/competition-schema.spec.ts`, and `describe('transactional outbox and durable dispatch (§4.12, SPK-AT-023)')` in `apps/api/src/kernel/__tests__/outbox.spec.ts`. Several titles name two ids separated by a slash ("SPK-AT-003 / SPK-CAP-005: ..."); both rows cite the test.
3. **File placement** (**Verified** practice, **Proposed** as a rule): module tests live in `apps/api/src/modules/<module>/__tests__/<topic>.spec.ts` (API tests with `createTestApp()`) and next to the code for pure rules (`<module>.state-machines.spec.ts`, `time/recurrence.spec.ts`, `pricing/discount-evaluator.spec.ts`); each module has a schema-invariant file (`identity-schema.spec.ts`, `catalog-schema.spec.ts`, `competition-schema.spec.ts`, scheduling and facilities `schema-invariants.spec.ts`) whose tests show the database rejecting the violation (`isUniqueViolation`, `isExclusionViolation`, or a check-constraint or trigger error from `@sportspark/db`). Acceptance journeys are proven today inside the owning module's `__tests__` (section 9, item 13); a dedicated `apps/api/src/__tests__/journeys/spk-at-###-<slug>.spec.ts` directory for journeys that span more than two modules is **Proposed** and does not exist.
4. **Evidence must be a passing test, cited by file and title.** A screen, a README, a comment or a type alone never moves a row past Specified (§1.5: "A visible screen is not evidence that its underlying workflow is complete"). A source citation of the id without a test named after it moves a row to "Implemented" at most (regeneration step 3).
5. **Stage transitions** (**Proposed**):
   - Specified to Implemented: code merged under the owning module with the id cited in comments and the commit message (CLAUDE.md), but no test named after the id yet, or only generic tests.
   - Implemented to Verified (slice): a test named after the id passes in `pnpm test`; the evidence column names the clauses it covers and the clauses that remain Specified.
   - Verified (slice) to Verified (module): tests named after the id cover every clause of the requirement as worded; for concurrency-sensitive rows, the `test-postgres` CI job has run and its result is recorded in the release evidence ledger.
   - Verified to Operator accepted: the §6.3 or §6.2 acceptance task is completed by the named operator and recorded in the release evidence ledger (§6.7) with build and environment (§6.8 item 11).
   - Operator accepted to Released: the release owner (§6.8 item 12) records the release in the ledger.
   - "Slice" qualifiers are removed only when the whole requirement, not a clause, meets the stage.
6. **Status column discipline**: a row stays **Pending decision** until the decision register records the answer as "answered" with an owner and effective date (§6.6); "unknown" is never rewritten as "not applicable". A **Verified** marker never removes a **Pending decision** marker on the same row: a mechanism can be proven while its operator values are unknown (SPK-FAC-001, SPK-SCHED-001, SPK-PAY-003).
7. **Package column** changes only when the sponsor approves a change to the §6.5 sequence; the `*` marker is removed when §6.5 itself names the capability.
8. **Legacy column** is filled only from the original specification once it is in the repository (section 8).
9. **Review trigger**: every pull request that cites an `SPK` id or a `§4.7` invariant (CLAUDE.md requires the citation) updates the affected rows in the same pull request. A pull request that lands a module (a new migration range in use, a README moving from "scaffold" to "implemented") also updates section 3, section 6 and the counts in section 7.

### 10.2 Regeneration procedure (**Proposed**, commands are the ones used to build the 2026-10-24 version; the 2026-10-30 regeneration indexed the same `it`/`describe`/`test` titles over `git ls-files` of `*.spec.ts`, `*.test.ts` and `*.test.tsx`, expanding `/` and `..` id lists and reading "§4.7 invariant n" forms)

```
# 1. Ids the specification defines (must equal the rows in section 4, plus §4.7 (1)..(12))
grep -oE 'SPK-[A-Z]+-[0-9]{3}' docs/spec/master-specification.md | sort -u

# 2. Ids and sections that tests name (must equal the evidence column and section 6);
#    run from the repository root so that .claude/worktrees and node_modules are not scanned
grep -rnoE 'SPK-[A-Z]+-[0-9]{3}|§4\.7 \([0-9]+\)|§4\.7 invariant [0-9]+|§[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?' \
  --include='*.spec.ts' --exclude-dir=node_modules --exclude-dir=dist apps packages

# 2b. Test titles verbatim, one line per describe/it, with the per-file count
for f in $(find apps packages -name '*.spec.ts' -not -path '*/node_modules/*' | sort); do
  echo "== $f ($(grep -cE '^\s*(it|test)\(' "$f") tests)"; grep -nE "^\s*(describe|it|test)\(" "$f"; done

# 3. Ids that source code cites but no test names (candidates for "Implemented", never "Verified")
grep -rnoE 'SPK-[A-Z]+-[0-9]{3}' --include='*.ts' --exclude='*.spec.ts' \
  --exclude-dir=node_modules --exclude-dir=dist apps packages

# 4. Which modules are implemented (README status) and which migrations exist
grep -m1 -nE 'Status' apps/api/src/modules/*/README.md; ls packages/db/migrations

# 5. Confirm the evidence still passes before publishing an update
pnpm test
```

Differences between step 1 and section 4 mean a specification change: add rows. Differences between step 2 and the evidence column mean a stale matrix: fix the rows, never the tests. Any test in step 2 that names an id but fails removes "Verified" from that row until it passes again. The `.claude/worktrees` directory, when present, holds other working copies and must not be scanned: run the commands on `apps` and `packages` only, as written.

### 10.3 Related Package A documents

`docs/README.md` names the companion artifacts (role and permission matrix, decision register, integration register, authority matrix, journeys, acceptance fixtures, metric dictionary, native acceptance matrix, release evidence ledger). The §6.6 owners cited in this document's Status column are the ones `docs/package-a/decision-register.md` carries, and the `DEC-###` identifiers cited in section 4 are its records. The entity-level and endpoint-level statuses this document summarises are `docs/architecture/domain-model.md` and `docs/architecture/api-and-events.md`; at the time of writing both still describe the competition module as a scaffold or Proposed, while this document follows the tree (competition part 1 landed with migration 0400 and seven test files on 2026-10-24). Whichever document is updated later must be reconciled with section 3.
